## Undead beast

An undead beast was a very rare type of undead that was from both Toril and Krynn.

Description
Undead beasts were non-intelligent quadrupedal creatures that killed without a thought. They were about the size of small dragons and had exposed ribcages and torn, rotting flesh. Their heads and backs had several horns protruding from them. Despite usually walking on their hands and legs, they could also rear onto their hind legs.

Combat
Undead beasts attacked with their powerful, fleshy arms and slashed with their beastly claws.

Appendix
Trivia
 The two types of undead beasts, the stahnk and the gholor, were originally from the Dragonlance campaign setting. The anhkolox is a green-glowing burning-hot variety, though the 5-edition Dragonlance: Shadow of the Dragon Queen applied this name to all undead beasts to regular stahnk undead beasts instead.

Appearances

References

## Untamo

Untamo was an outsider deity of sleep and dreams.

Personality
Untamo was very mild mannered in everything he did. He heavily disliked being disturbed, such as by being summoned, no matter what the reason. He would often punish those who dared to disturb him.

Abilities
Untamo radiated an aura of sleep that would cause any creatures who had not recently slept for thirteen hours or more to instantly fall into a slumber when in his presence.

Combat
This deity typically fought with an enchanted club that could cause those struck by it to fall asleep.

Realm
Untamo resided within the plane of Concordant Opposition, otherwise known as the Outlands, in a realm known as the Sleeping Lands. He was rarely known to wander beyond his domain. Whoever entered his domain was said to instantly fall within Untamo's dream and could only escape if he willed it.

His domain was adjacent to those of two other outsider deities, one of them being Yen-Wang-Yeh, a god who was worshiped by a small cult in Kozakura.

Activities
Any being that summoned Untamo had to pledge to serve him and his needs for an entire year in order to receive his aid in any matter. Those agree to his terms were usually granted the ability to cast the spell sleep four times per day.

History
In the kingdom of Bakar, a survivor state of the Imaskar Empire, a mighty city known as Medinat Muskawoon housed temples to gods of faiths that the populace's ancestors brought over from their original worlds. Among these temples was one dedicated to Untamo.

Worshipers
The priestly vestments of Untamo's faith included a blue tunic.

References

Undead bane weapon was a transmutation spell used by paladins. This spell originated from Kelemvor, the god of the dead.

Effects
This spell enchanted a weapon to cause additional harm against the undead. This spell worked on melee weapons and up to 50 arrows or other projectile weapons.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required a weapon to cast.

## Undead familiar

Undead familiar was a necromancy spell used by evil wizards. This spell was found in Kyristan's Mysteries and was developed by the lich Kyristan.

Effects
This spell allowed a particularly vile spellcaster to create a familiar out of any corpse, including those of humans and demihumans. The corpse could be in any state of decay. The created zombie or skeleton functioned like others of its kind but possessed minimal intelligence, allowing it to better serve the spellcaster. The spellcaster was able to issue mental commands at a distance of up to  thanks to an empathic link with the undead familiar.

If separated from the spellcaster for any reason the undead familiar began to slowly deteriorate until it was destroyed. Spellcasters possessed a strong bond with their undead familiar, so if the familiar perished there was a small chance the caster would die as well, or at least suffer a permanent weakening of their vitality.

Undead familiars could be turned normally, but if within sight of the caster they were turned as if they were wights. These familiars could not be destroyed by turning.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required a corpse or skeleton and a silver ring placed on one of the familiar's fingers to cast.

Appendix
References

## Undercliff

Undercliff was a rural community just outside the walls of Waterdeep, located east of the city proper. Along with the Field Ward, it was considered an unofficial ward of the city.

Description
Undercliff was a wide area of rolling grassland, with a few wooded areas and several farms, orchards, and areas dedicated to animal husbandry.

Geography

Upon exiting the River Gate in the Trades Ward, River Street continued into Undercliff Way, which ran north of Undercliff through several farms and a small village until connecting with the High Road north of the North Gate. Before turning north, the road bifurcated, and its southern branch, known as Southcliff Way, served the southern farms before connecting with the High Road south of the South Gate.

Trade
Undercliff was famous for its various inns, which attracted many visitors and were exempt from the taxation that businesses in the city proper were subject to. Businesses in Undercliff were also outside of the purview of the Guilds of Waterdeep and the region was not formally protected by the City Watch.

History
By 1479 DR, increasing numbers of dwarves had begun moving from the Field Ward to Undercliff, intending to tunnel into the cliff face and build new homes directly beneath Waterdeep proper. Separate from those of the Warrens, these tunnels broke into sewers and basements, and drew the ire of the Masked Lords who sent patrols of hired adventurers to stop any further mining.

In the, a group of scarecrows began terrorizing the local population and killing livestock. The Emerald Enclave sent a group of members to deal with the threat.

Notable Locations
 Amendsfarm, a prison farm operated by the City Watch, where Waterdhavians convicted of minor offenses were sentenced to pay hard labor for a limited period of time.
 The Marching Grounds, a well protected training camp of the City Guard.
 Snobeedle Orchard and Meadery, an orchard, shop, and drinking hall operated by the Snobeedle family of halflings. Tourists were able to pick their own fruit during the appropriate seasons.

Inhabitants
The area of Undercliff was largely inhabited by halflings and gnomes. Most of the buildings in the area were in fact sized for its small inhabitants.

The Amcathra family held an estate in Undercliff, where they trained horses bred in Amphail and sold them to the City Guard. The Hothemer family also held a villa in the region, where they conducted business outside of Waterdhavian jurisdiction.

The University of Suzail was an institution of learning in the city of Suzail, the capital of the kingdom of Cormyr, in the mid–14 century DR.

The academic staff engaged in research, teaching, and writing, and usually writing when they wearied of teaching. The professors were well-respected experts in their respective fields who wrote books of their own. The majority of masters at the university had a job for life.

## Uncrimmon's Trophies & Sundries

Uncrimmon's Trophies & Sundries was a Mosstonian store that bought and sold curious of interest, usually catering to adventurers, alchemists, wizards, and traveling clerics.

Location
Uncrimmon's Trophies & Sundries was located at the western wall of Mosstone, beyond a sharp-turned offshoot of the Carldeir Lane. The store's unusual-looking tall oval door was painted in a color of dark dried blood that glowed in unsettling green radiance after dark. A small sign outside was the only indication that Uncrimmon's, in fact, was a store.

Interior
Uncrimmon's usually had very few customers inside the store. The store's walls were covered in countless open drawers and shelves that often reminded visitors of open coffins. The shelves had arrangements of large bottles and slopping drawers held human skeletons.

Services
Said human bones, as well as anything inside the bottles and jars, was available for purchase. Uncrimmon's catered to a very specific clientele who was interesting in dealing in body parts of exotic creatures, pickled, dried, and fresh, that ranged from whole bats to mind flayer face tentacles.

Apart from macabre supplies, the store carried sleeping potions, a wide variety of poisons and venoms, antidotes, rare living spiders, tiny mimics, tarrasque teeth, sahuagin scales, and many other supplies.

Inhabitants
Ardreth Ulnshoor was the storekeep in the 14 century DR. Ardreth was considered creepy by locals who thought him to be able to read minds and be an illithid in disguise. In reality, he was a doppelganger, the fact that was confirmed by local Harpers. Ardreth's true nature was a secret to most, and rumors of him being a mind flayer kept most untrustworthy elements out of the store.

Ardreth Ulnshoor did not tolerate customers stealing or attempting to swindle him. Occasionally, he had to use his medallion of ESP and shapeshifting abilities to deal with ne'er-do-well and cheats. Apart from that, he liked to get to know his clients with a bit of psionic probing.

Apart from the storekeep himself, the store was guarded by two floating armored torsos, helmed horrors, that floated above the entrance and followed shoppers inside with their swords drawn. The horrors were able to generate magic spells with their sinister glowing eyes, and other parts of the store were enchanted to hurl and deflect powerful magics.

The last "resident" of the store was a floating human eye and a floating human hand that assisted Ardreth Ulnshoor running the store. These magical floating body parts, in reality, were instruments through which the store's true owner, Uncrimmon, operated. Uncrimmon was a powerful wizardly beholder who laired somewhere in the forests outside of Mosstone. Through the eye, the powerful aberration was able to see, hear, and talk, and through the hand, Uncrimmon was able to cast spells, hold and carry kings, and even clumsily wield smaller weapons, like a dagger.

Rumors & Legends
Local tales of Mosstone often spoke of the skeletons that were purely displayed in Uncrimmon's Trophies & Sundries. Locals wintered and gossiped about them actually being animated undead guardians of the store. However, that was likely nothing but tall tales.
Appendix
References

---

## Uncrimmon

Uncrimmon was a mysterious proprietor of the expensive curio shop, Uncrimmon's Trophies & Sundries, in Mosstone, Tethyr.

Abilities
Uncrimmon was a secretive beholder that possessed powerful arcane magics. The creature was never seen in the open. Instead, it operated through a floating human eyeball and a floating severed human hand. The aberration could talk, hear, and see through the ensorcelled organ, freely moving it around his store. Uncrimmon used the floating hand to help Ardreth Ulnshoor run the store. The hand could carry, grab, strangle, and hold objects. It was able to wield weapons clumsily but was very apt with small and sharp blades that beholder preferred. Additionally, the mysterious store owner was able to cash his powerful magics through the floating hand.

Possessions
The eye tyrant owned the store and everything inside. Every human skeleton, every pickled bat, every poison. This enterprise was protected by the storekeep, Ardreth Ulnshoor, secretly a doppelganger who served the many-eyed aberration seemingly out of fear. The beholder's store also had two fearsome helmed horrors who floated after customers with their swords drawn in case of trouble. Like other of his kin, Uncrimmon was paranoid and greedy, so he enchanted the store itself to reflect magics and produce powerful spells to protect the eye tyrant's business and overly-priced stock.

History
Uncrimmon's Trophies & Sundries and its strange owner's ages were unknown. However, the beholder was well established in the 14 century DR. Volothamp Geddarm was able to uncover the store owner's identity and theorized that Uncrimmon used his store to keep an eye on adventurers, wizards, and traveling clerics for some unknown and sinister party.
Appendix
References

## Unseelie

Unseelie were a kind of malevolent sprite who enjoyed harming others.

Description
Though roughly humanoid in appearance, unseelie features were usually exaggerated and grotesque. Examples included elongated arms, large noses, and other twisted features. Most had tattered insect wings. Unseelie could alter their size between  and .

Combat
All unseelie could become invisible whenever they wished. They enjoyed ambushing and terrorizing their victims. They used small weapons in combat coated with a poison capable of inducing sleep in their targets. Those unlucky enough to fall victim to this poison found themselves bound and tortured upon waking.

Unseelie could cast a single spell once per day. They usually preferred casting spells that caused harm to others.

Society

Sadistic by nature, unseelie took great pleasure from harming or even killing any who wandered into their territory. Even victims able to escape often did so with polymorphed appendages. Unseelie preferred to dwell in dark places like swamps, bogs, and twisted forests.

These creatures did not have a structured society. Anarchy reigned supreme among the unseelie with each individual behaving as they wished. The unseelie palaces were located on an invisible demiplane.

History
The unseelie were locked in a seemingly endless war with their cousins, the seelie. Conflicts between the two occurred in the Yuirwood of Aglarond.

Ecology
Though unseelie acquired their sustenance from their home demiplane, they tormented local animal populations purely for the joy of it. Likewise, they enjoyed felling trees, damaging vegetation, and poisoning fresh water for sport.

Unseelie inhabited mountain passes around the land of Halruaa where they lured travelers off paths into perilous wilderness. Stories of the dark fey folk were common among the inhabitants of the region.

## Unnamed drow operative

Referred to as the mysterious drow or the Bregan D'aerthe Operative, an unnamed drow of the Bregan D'aerthe mercenary organization was active in Neverwinter in the late 15 century DR.

Activities
He recruited fellow drow to help him foil the efforts of House Xorlarrin in undermining the rebuilding of Neverwinter, telling them that, if nothing else, it was for the good of Neverwinter. He declined to say what was in it for him or Bregan D'aerthe, however. He could usually be found somewhere propped up against a wall, loitering, and possibly even lurking.

History
Following the Siege of Neverwinter in the, this drow went to the Blacklake District and hired a drow adventurer to raid a Nashers-held house and kill a Xorlarrin agent who was meeting with them to provide magical weapons and equipment.

He later learned of a Xorlarrin spy working with the Blackdagger Bandits, again to provide magical weapons and aid but this time with the deal already done. While he stayed at the Broken Crown inn, he had the drow adventurer go to the Raven Cliff Beach to disrupt the delivery and destroy the weapons, to make it seem as if the Xorlarrin had double-crossed them. Afterward, the Xorlarrin spy came out of hiding to meet with the Blackdaggers to repair relations, just as the operative planned. He sent the adventurer to slay the spy and her contacts in the abandoned mineshaft they met in.

## Unnamed cloaked man

A cloaked man, who signed his messages as "a friend", was an unnamed agent active in Neverwinter in the late 15 century DR.

History
Following the Siege of Neverwinter in the, the cloaked man worked for an unidentified power in Neverwinter who desired for people to resettle the devastated Blacklake District. To this end, he worked to disrupt and eliminate the gangs that plagued it, the Nashers and the Dead Rats, by turning them against each other. He hired a rogue adventurer to raid a Dead Rats–held house, kill the wererats, and Nashers propaganda pamphlets by a bedroll to make it appear that a double agent was behind it.

Later, in Neverdeath graveyard, he asked a rogue to recover a Lucanis family heirloom from Gorim Tomb-Raider.

Finally, the cloaked man learned of a plot to assassinate Lord Dagult Neverember, by a group better equipped than the Nashers. Resolving to deal with it quietly to avoid embarrassing the city's ruler, the cloaked man located their hideout in Protector's Enclave and hired an adventurer to enter through a window and defeat the conspirators.

## Unity

Unity was a Netherese city during the time of ancient Netheril.

History
Unity was founded in the early 600s NY (around ) and served to defend against goblins.

Unity was the site for the largest of all temples constructed for the glory of Amaunator—the Forested Enclave of the Face of the Sun. This towering structure soared 17 stories into the sky and was visible for miles around.

In, Unity was successfully attacked by the bandit lord Olostin, starting his 33 year reign of terror.

In, the city was besieged by extra-planar fiends, who waged a three-year war for the city before being banished or killed by Karsus.

In the spring of, magically mutated creatures began coming out of the Far Horns Forest near Unity, believed to be caused by Karsus's use of heavy magic during the war against the fiends.

## Universal solvent

Universal solvent, also known as ultimate solution, was a magical liquid that was similar to a potion or magical substance.

Powers
This item appeared to have the properties of both oil of slipperiness and a potion of delusion if examined with magic. What a dose of universal solvent would actually do was to dissolve any adhesive or sticky material that it was applied to. This included cement and magical substances such as sovereign glue, as well as the goo from a tanglefoot bag and the adhesive made by kuo-toa.

A single ounce of universal solvent would work on  of adhesive material. A typical container held  of fluid. If distilled to one-third of its normal potency, one ounce would cause  of organic and inorganic material to disintegrate.

Creation
Creating it required skill at crafting magical items and the spell disintegrate.

Notable Owners
Individuals
 Torm, a member of the Knights of Myth Drannor who was a notorious hoarder of magic devices and trinkets, owned some ultimate solution and sometimes kept it on his person.
Religions
 Goldeyes, specialty priests of the Church of Waukeen, had a chance of receiving a vial of universal solvent as one of the miscellaneous gifts they received upon admission to the clergy.

Unique Mageries was an arcane spellbook penned by Nezram, a wizard who traveled the planes extensively. It contained 21 spells ranging from common to unique or rare.

Unique Mageries was a large tome that measured four handwidths wide by ten handwidths tall. Polished ivory formed both the front and rear cover. Each cover was decorated with inlaid ebony. Each of the 21 pages was gilt-edged and held a single spell.

Powers
Unique Mageries contained the following spells:
magic missile • stinking cloud • wizard lock • dispel magic • feign death • gust of wind • infravision • tongues • water breathing • fear • fumble • polymorph self • remove curse • wall of sand • wizard eye • telekinesis • anti-magic shell • Nezram's ruby ray • spelltrap • power word, blind • spellstrike

Nezram left the tome behind on Toril sometime during the 7 century DR when he traveled to another plane and never returned. It was taken from Nezram's tower by the green dragon Chathuulandroth who held it within his lair for 600 years before adventurers from the Black Gauntlet seized it from him. ## Underdark landwyrm

Underdark landwyrms, which were sometimes called cave dragons, were a species of landwyrm endemic to the Underdark.

Description
Underdark landwyrms were smaller than other landwyrms but still quite large. They had dark grey scales.

Personality
Underdark landwyrms were said to be evil creatures.

Abilities
Underdark landwyrms, like many other dragons, possessed a fear-inducing aura with a  radius. Unlike many other dragons, they had poor vision and hearing, instead relying on a combination of scent and echolocation to pinpoint creatures within  of them.

Underdark landwyrms were capable of exhaling a cloud of obscuring mist thrice each day. Their claws were known to leave nasty bleeding wounds, and they were skilled swimmers.

Combat
Underdark landwyrms preferred to cloak their approach with the obscuring mist they exhaled, before suddenly ambushing prey. They typically retreated after merely injuring foes, relying on the wounds they inflicted with their claws to fell them.

Ecology
Underdark landwyrms spent their entire existence in the darkness below ground, where they made elaborate lairs that took advantage of natural surroundings such as chasms and lakes. They were known to be partial to gifts of magic.

Landwyrms were among the dragons affected by the Dracorage.

Undead battlemight was a spell used by priestesses of Lolth.

When cast, this spell gave skeletons and zombies the same mobility as the caster. Both the natural agiltity and speed of the undead creatures were temporarily increased. During casting, the caster had to touch the undead for the spell to take effect.

## Underdungeon

The Underdungeon was an unexplored series of tunnels that were found during the delving of Stardeep's foundations. They were widely considered too dangerous to visit. When Stardeep was secreted away to a demiplane, the Underdungeon somehow still connected it to Sildëyuir.

Location
The tunnel complex was found beneath Stardeep.

Structure
The structure was entirely below the surface. The only entrances were one guarded by the Edgewarden in Sildëyuir, and one on the Parade Hall of Stardeep.

Interior
One of the locations was a refuse pit directly beneath the Grand Vestibule, where the victims of a floor trap were led.

From the Sildëyuir side, a fungal garden lit up with faintly glowing blue spores was eventually accessible. One of its tunnels was full of revived fossils that might attack intruders. The local fungus hulks were not in friendly terms with the fossils. Another passage led to a chasm where a stone spider with a voice like a crying baby hunted sometimes. Despite the spider being made of stone, the zone was thick with cobwebs. One could arrive at Callambea there on a sprint.

The tunnels were closest to sedimentary stones such as salt and gypsum. There were numerous side passages; one of them in particular could accomodate five riders side by side. From the Stardeep side, the passage led to a screen of gold and green; though it was visually an obstacle, its magic was spent. It led to a giant chamber in the white stone, open to the sky, with only a giant pair of metallic double doors layered with coppery metal on the other side. Past those doors, a basalt passage across the white, bone-white rock spanning a few miles before reaching an underground ruined city, later christened Callambea.

Callambea
Both passages converged into a city eventually. From the outside in, the city was full of squat tenements of white stone. Closer to the center, there were balusters, fluted columns, and slender balconies. The ground was nonetheless littered with the fossilized or perhaps bodies of who seemed to have been the inhabitants. The central city hub had those petrified corpses as thick as cobbles. The ceiling arched into a vault with a gigantic violet light; beneath the flashing light, there was a pyramid of bodies, perhaps  high, with a ruby throne on which the relic lich sat as of . That same year, after the defeat of the lich, Telarian named the locale "Callambea".

In one point, the path from Callambea to the Underdungeon had a wide tranche with slick walls, and at least one space on the higher part of those walls where one could observe people returning.

History
The Underdungeon was found while delving the foundations of Stardeep. While both entrances were known to lead through the same complex, nobody had actually made the trip between both; there were only sporadic investigations, such as one by Telarian and Brathtar. Nonetheless, it was known that the Underdungeon could connect Stardeep and Sildëyuir and that its tunnels extended to the edges of Sildëyuir on at least two places. Through the whole existence of Stardeep, nothing from the Underdungeon had come out to cause trouble, leading to the entrance on Stardeep's side staying unprotected save for a single instance of Cynosure. Conversely, no force from Stardeep had ever investigated the area in depth.

On, Telarian and Kiril Duskmourn both crossed the Underdungeon hoping to find Kiril and an entrance to Stardeep, respectively. They met at the ruined city, where they were attacked by a relic lich; after the battle, Telarian named the zone Callambea.

Inhabitants
The Underdungeon was known to be full of deadly, implacable foes. There were fungus hulks and at least one stone spider. There were also numerous revived fossils inside the Underdungeon; they seemed to answer to the relic lich that resided there. The nature of those undead was unknown.

Rumours & Legends
The few knights that had managed to return from Stardeep often spoke of pyramids of living stone, haunting whispers, and ancient beings, older then Sildëyuir.

The Keeper of the Cerulean Sign Telarian, while sifting for clues as to the nature of the city, received a vision from his sword, of a tower burning as it receeded into the sky, expelling a giant cloud of darkness over the city whose center he'd later name Callambea. The same sword claimed that the fossils belonged to a murdered species.

## Unholy (weapon quality)

Unholy weapons were those imbued or otherwise enchanted with evil power that was especially harmful when wielded against morally-upstanding foes.

Powers
Unholy weapons inflicted more grievous wounds whenever they struck a good-aligned creature or being. Similarly, any godly person that attempted to pick up an unholy weapon themselves were severely weakened until they let it go.

Notable Owners
 Malkizid, wielded a +2 silver unholy bastard sword.

Notable Unholy Weapons
 Skull blades, enchanted longswords wielded by members of the Cyricist faith.

## Unseelie Court (divine realm)

The Unseelie Court was the divine realm of the Queen of Air and Darkness, one of the most powerful Archfey in all the Feywild.

Description
The Unseelie Court was a mirror opposite of the Seelie Court; where the latter was a place of laughter and friendship, the Unseelie Court was a place of hatred, enslavement, and death. The Court was located in an immense cavern of black stone full of stalagmites and stalactites, centered around a single mountainous stalagmite upon which the Queen's black onyx throne stood. The Black Diamond itself rested upon the throne.

The Unseelie Court appeared macabre and twisted to visitors: trees grew in unnatural shapes, shadows hid blood-soaked alcoves, and nightshade and blood wort plants glowed to provide the Court with haphazard red light. The throne itself was shaped like a great dragon.

Cosmography
Different accounts placed the Unseelie Court either within Phlegethon, Pandemonium's dark third layer, or within the Feywild.

Notable Locations
There were no notable locations in the Unseelie Court beyond the throne of the Queen of Air and Darkness; no towns or other settlements were built there.

Inhabitants
The most notable inhabitant of the Unseelie Court was the Queen of Air and Darkness; as she no longer manifested a physical form, her presence filled the cavern, emanating from the throne and Black Diamond.

All of the inhabitants of the Unseelie Court lived to serve the Queen. When not on missions assigned them by their queen, they typically lived around the stalagmite holding the throne. Outside of these missions, they typically had little care for or about creatures on other planes.

Although the majority of inhabitants were various evil or corrupted fey, any creature with even a drop of fey blood, or with useful or entertaining skills, could be welcome as well. Undead creatures were used as menial servants. Yeth hounds and hell hounds were among the favorite servants of the Queen, and more successful fey servants were sometimes rewarded with one. Specific types of fey known to live there included brambles, quicklings,> spriggans, and hoary hunters.

## Undead focus

Undead focus was a spell used by clerics of Lolth.

Effects
This spell allowed the caster to use an undead creature as a spell focus. When successfully cast, the caster could then cast a further spell through the undead creature. While the spell is funneled through the undead creature, the caster still had to memorize and cast the spell including components. As long as the caster and the undead creature are on the same plane, the caster could funnel a spell from anywhere. The amount of spells a caster could use via the focus was determined by the caster's skill level, or until the undead creature was destroyed.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required a material component, a drop of the the caster's blood.

## Underglass

A greenhouse, hothouse, glasshouse or conservatory was a building with extensive windows to let sunlight in, and a structure to trap heat inside, typically used to grow plants that were too sensitive to cold or outside conditions. They were called underglass in the Realms.

In the Realms
Worshipers of Silvanus sometimes maintained underglass conservatories to manage demand for plants otherwise found only on the wild. Greenhouses were often used to provide plants for Greengrass festivals.

Some wizards used their laboratories as hothouses from time to time. Savants who worked with flora sometimes made use of greenhouses. The sage Quillan in particular was considering building a greenhouse around .

The city of Furthinghome in the Unapproachable East was famous for its glasshouses. Neverwinter used many hothouses to grow flowers all-year-round. Corellon's Crown in Waterdeep was a glasshouse in the late 15 century DR. The selkie Ivain, a priestess of Lathander, had also created a glasshouse of her own near Waterdeep in the mid-14 century DR, though the frost giant Snorri destroyed it. Wolfhill House, built by Hezekiel Wolf around the mid-11 century DR, also had a conservatory. The necromancer Fairfern had a greenhouse in Tsurlagol.

The Dome of the Moon was a very peculiar glasshouse, which kept its plants at about 60ºF.

Other
Alusair Obarskyr was once described as a "hothouse flower".

Appearances

## Unseelie nymph

An unseelie nymph was a twisted relative of the nymph that delighted in the destruction of natural beauty.

Description
Their beauty, while rivaling that of their good cousins, was not quite the same; only the occasional glint of malevolence in their eyes or smirk betrayed their intentions, and it was easy to miss. Unseelie nymphs that favored forests had black or brown hair, while those that favored aquatic environments had blond or blue-green hair.

Personality
It delighted unseelie nymphs to witness and to foment the destruction of all that was good and beautiful. Their hearts were some of the most corrupt in nature, intensely spiteful and willing to destroy anything beautiful so long as their own beauty wasn't at risk. They maintained an innocent demeanor at all times, never overtly betraying their true colors.

Unseelie nymphs were extremely particular about their chosen environment; a forest unseelie nymph would stay away from lakes (even those she befouled), and an aquatic unseelie nymph kept her distance from forests, never dwelling willingly in the other's environment.

Abilities
Any creature that laid eyes on the unseelie nymph was at risk of falling in love with her, an effect well likened to being charmed; men so affected were known to be more vulnerable to the nymph's corruption and especially pliable to her orders. This effect was so intense that even elves were at a fair risk of being afflicted. However, the most dramatic abilities of this kind of nymph were their passive effects on the wilderness around them; they fouled water, twisted vegetation, and even caused them to die. By, the areas were measured as  after four months; by, the area was more precisely measured as a circle with a radius of  per week. Some intelligent plants were able to endure this, but surviving for a week was not a guarantee they'd survive the next; even if they felt the damage, they would not necessarily know to attribute it to the nymph. Only the most powerful druids had the means to reverse this damage without a limited wish or wish spell.

Creatures that spent weeks charmed in the company of an unseelie nymph were degraded by its ability to corrupt beauty, afflicted by a mysterious wasting. Such creatures were permanently diminished in their fortitude and in their talents, due to the nymph's influence; it was thought that a wish or limited wish only could reverse this damage, though restoration was partially effective. By, they were known to do this actively, and the damage was considered to be centered in their force of personality rather than talents. While it was possible, albeit taxing, to endure this effect, it was especially vicious when used against men.

Forest-dwelling nymphs were excellent climbers, while aquatic nymphs were excellent swimmers.

Combat
Unseelie nymphs preferred to leave combat to those they had charmed, using their dimension door to stay out of combat.

Society
Unseelie nymphs were the sworn enemy of druids, rangers, and servants of nature deities. They seldom banded together, but when they did, it was to destroy the work of a powerful druid or other servant of nature. Otherwise, they were solitary, keeping only the company of those they charmed and slowly drained of their beauty. They were extremely particular about their environment, and would not willingly dwell outside of their favored terrain.

Notable Unseelie Nymphs
Glynara, an unseelie nymph of Cormanthor in the late 13 century DR.

## Uncovering Umgatlik

Uncovering Umgatlik was a research manuscript dedicated to the study of the ancient Netherese lich Umgatlik.

Contents
Uncovering Umgatlik was a collection of drafts, manuscripts, and sketches, some of which were done in charcoal. The work was compiled by a hapless Savant Gumbril, who signed his manuscript as Savant Gumbril the Great.

History
Savant Gumbril discovered lore about what he thought was a long lost god of the ancient Netheril empire. He assembled a team and, eventually, uncovered Umgatlik's shrine, buried within the deadly cliffs of the Crab's Claw Coast in the . Uncovering Umgatlik was what Savant Gumbril titled his notes and copies of writings and arcane symbols collected from within the lost crypt. As of 1372 DR, the research remained unpublished. The book's future remained unclear, possibly due to Gumbril's rumored death or the research being discredited or even abandoned.

History
This ritual was used as a purification process of novices entering into Leira's clergy, by other priests rising in rank, or as a form of penance for slighting their faith (usually by telling the truth too often). The participant would walk down a column lined with other priests holding tall lit candles that were reflected by pools and mirrors.

## Una

Una was a kobold specialty priest, or morninglord, of Lathander in the late 14 century DR.

Description
At the age of fifteen, Una was  tall and weighted . She had piercing blue eyes and wore priestly robes of her god. Una was sensitive to ridicule and picked her garb very carefully, aware of how goofy a kobold in the clothing of a cleric would look.

Personality
She was a fast-talking animated woman, knowledgeable of etiquette and proficient in both healing and herbalism. She was a happy-go-lucky optimist overflowing with enthusiasm. She aspired to one day wear shining plate armor and saved every other meager coin to one day buy a custom-forged set, sending the rest of the money to her temple.

Abilities
As a morninglord, Una had access to all spells and miracles granted by her faith. She could turn undead and had a unique ability to conjure up faerie fire. Being a kobold, Una possessed infravision.

Possessions
Una wielded clubs and maces and defended herself with an enchanted +1 shield – gift of her temple's High Priest.

Her most valued possession was a recorder that Una played with little provocation. She loved music and hoped to find a teacher to help her master more instruments.

History
Una discovered her calling when a group of Lathanderian clergy was captured by a band of kobolds. The young kobold girl snuck into a hut where the humans were held and offered them a chance to escape in exchange for being taught the priestly ways of the Morninglord. One of the priests offered to take Una to a temple where she would be taught Lathander's teachings. The kobold girl chewed through the ropes that held humans and soon, all three men were free.

The Lathanderians honored the bargain and introduced Una to the temple's High Priest. After hearing her story, the High Priest agreed to teach the kobold, and, to everyone's surprise, Una eventually succeeded. She diligently studied her new religion and human culture. Some observers found Una's progress and rise through clergy ranks to be unnatural and threw racist comments the kobold's way. However, her fellow priests staunchly stood up for Una.

When Una successfully turned a vampire with her divine powers, the High Priest announced that she was ready to depart the temple and adventure, spreading Lathander's light onto others. The temple put out a notice, searching for a group of willing adventurers to take Una under their wing, preferably a group of followers of the Morninglord or a band that had a cleric of Lathander as its member.

## Unglur

Unglur was the middle of the three islands located west of Thelve and the the fortress library of Candlekeep. During the century that followed the Spellplague, its was a part of the so-called Cimarine Isles.

Description
Unlike its sister isles, Unglur had no surface ruins, and there were only a few sea caves that could be used for shelter or harborage. The island also lacked any freshwater springs. Vegetation was scarce and only consisted of thin shrubbery. Unglur had no harbors and just one anchorage at the center of the western shore, where the curved shore met with a snout. It was the only spot for boats to reach the beach despite strong blistering winds that pelted the shore. The winds made it almost impossible to un-beach any boats without the use of magic.

The island's southwestern shore was  long, and the short snout on the southeast had a  long shore. Continuing around the snout, its eastern part was  long until curving to the northeast for  and continuing curving to the northwest for  more.

Geography
Unglur was located to the west of Thelve, with two sister islands to the northwest – Sklorn to the east and Raerest to the west. Together Sklorn, Unglur, and Raerest were known as the Crab Reach, or simply the Reach among locals.

Unglur was separated from the isle of Sklorn by a  stretch of water and  from Unglur's eastern neighbor – Raerest.

Flora & Fauna
The island had a negligent population of seals, few monsters, and even fewer castaway inhabitants. The only creatures of note here were abundant gray coasters and rock gulls that left their white marks on the island's rocks.

History
By the late 14 century DR, Unglur was used by pirates as a harbor, and its shores were littered with derelict ships and wrecks. For many years there existed stories of an undead creature haunting the land. the Spirit of Unglur was a long-dead bloodthirsty berserker pirate turned a strange monstrous creature with long claws.

## Undreir Facilitations

Undreir Facilitations was a second-hand shop and fence in Tilverton in the 14 century DR, operated by the unscrupulous merchant Phidalpar Undreir.

Location
The shop sat in the southern edge of the Old Town of Tilverton on a side lane just off of Gateguard Road, opposite the new location of the Windlord's Rest.

Defenses
Phidalpar was not a member of the Rogues of Tilverton, and protected himself and his business with a dozen or more guards on-hand armed with sleep-poisoned darts and daggers in case of trouble.

Undreir Facilitations bought and sold all manner of goods, regardless of how questionable their origin or purpose. Phidalpar was always willing to take care of "awkward" items that adventurers brought him, and offered them "simple, little jobs" that needed taking care of.

## Unnamed South River village

This village was a small elven settlement located along the Tun River in the Tunlands of Cormyr.

Description
This unnamed elven village stood on the southwestern shores of the Tun River, west of Skull Crag and just south of the Tun Marshes. The river brought a cool refreshing breeze to the town, as well as giant crocodiles from upstream. The local population was known for its sharpshooting skills, with regular archery competitions taking place, open exclusively to the residents.

History
In the early 1360s DR, the elven village became the target for the lizarfolk tribe, allied with a group of young black dragons. The village started being attacked by the scaled forces from the nearby Tun Marshes. Subsequently, the monsters dealt some significant damage to the town, leaving it in a bad state of disrepair and vulnerable to continued assault. Despite their best attempts, elf and human archers failed to stop the attackers while the defenders slowly mounted the losses. A band of adventurers who were aiding Skull Crag in the time of need arrived just in time to stand with the village's defenses against yet another lizardfolk, dragon, and giant crocodile assault. Once the village was safe, the group ventured into the swamp in search of the lizardfolk camp nearby the settlement of Nyka's Rest on the convergence of two major Tun River tributaries.

Notable Locations
The village had a trader of various goods, including some elven magic times, a humble but busy tavern, and a boathouse, closed to outsiders. Those unwise to break in were faced with elven ghostly spirit guardians. The village had at least one shrine open to visitors, dedicated to the goddess Lliira. The town's harbor was littered with fishing boats and fishers who used nets to haul in the catch.

The town was also the site of the Grove of the Holy Ones, a local landmark where the benediction of the gods ceremony took place. Locals sold tickets to the event..

Inhabitants
The village was a mostly elven settlement, inhabited by some bow-wielding humans. The elven population kept a tight social group, while some inhabitants thought the south river region was not the same since the hin had left across the mountains.

## Unidentified disease (Vilhon Reach)

An unidentified disease affecting the skin, body, and mind broke out in the Vilhon Reach in the late 1350s DR.

Effects
The disease affected its victims in multiple ways. First, they felt drowsy and weak, with a slight loss of strength. Second, they had difficulties concentrating and could not memorize anything, including names and messages, and especially spells. But the most noticeable and unappealing symptom was the green-white patches resembling lichen that appeared on the skin.

No natural cure was found, bar a cure disease spell. The effects did fade after 2 to 5 months.

History
Heretofore unknown, there was an outbreak of the strange disease in the lands around the Vilhon Reach in the late 1350s DR. It spread quickly, but a cure was yet to be found.

Usage
Unusually, the alchemist Thoround of Nimpeth reported a use for the bodily fluids, that is, the spittle or blood, of humans affected by the disease. If the fluid was used in the making of ink for writing a forget spell, then the process could be made simpler and cheaper. Moreover, if the fluid was used in the ink for any spell, then the spell would disappear from the page upon casting from a spellbook in the normal manner—but would spontaneously reappear between 3 and 12 days later, provided the page remained intact.

Appendix

 Panicked Plague

## Unnamed dao (Undergate)

The unnamed dao of Undergate was a servant of the so-called Master of Undergate in the late 14 century DR.

Description
This particular dao was a giant elemental creature of earth, who towered over most inhabitants of the Undergate. He appeared typical to the rest of his people. However, his body language communicated the man was thoroughly deflated. The dao had somewhat crude orcish features and a balding head. He wore ivory-colored robes.

Personality
Despite his servitude, the dao retained his sarcastic sense of humor and regularly bad-mouthed his tentacled master.

History
Before becoming an inhabitant of Westgate, the dao was a noble genie from the Elemental Plane of Earth. He had one too many drinks one night and challenged Kajeel, the psion Master of Undergate, to a game of cards. The confident genie bet a hundred years of servitude against the psion's rowdy tavern underneaths the streets of Westgate. To no surprise, gambling against a creature who could read minds was a bad idea, and the unnamed dao lost. He was tasked with guarding the magical gate that led into Kajeel's establishment, allowing only those with a special token in, and only those especially picked by the illithid psion received the tokens.

## Unbinding

Unbinding was a powerful spell that could negate the effects of other spells.

Effects
When cast, a sphere of magical force materialized around the caster and moved with them. This sphere had a mobile nature and possessed the ability to unravel and nullify various forms of magical bindings, with a few exceptions. All spells that created seals or enclosures were instantly dispelled by the magical effect of this sphere.

This spell had the power to negate charm spells, hold spells of all types, wizard locks, and similar closures. It could dismantle physical or magical barriers, guards and wards, temporal stasis, slow, time stop, and other similar enchantments. It also terminated the effects of statue and shattered a magic jar, permanently destroying it and extinguishing the life force within. Even a creature imprisoned beneath the caster's feet was liberated as if a freedom spell had been cast. Additionally, all spells that maintain magical effects, including other spells, released their effects.

However, protective spells such as protection from evil, shield, minor globe of invulnerability, globe of invulnerability, and similar enchantments remain unaffected by this spell. Petrified creatures were not revealed or restored, and individuals bound to servitude, including familiars, invisible stalkers, genies, and elementals, were not freed. An antimagic shell remained impervious to the effects of this spell, and the sphere could not penetrate it. Protective circles and similar runic constructions that served as confinements were only affected if they held a creature captive. In such cases, the bindings were erased, or if physically impossible, they became ineffective except as mere decorations.

Curses, quests, and geas spells were nullified by this spell only if the caster was as strong or stronger than the original caster. How the spell reacted with artifacts and relics varied.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required material components, a lodestone and a pinch of saltpeter.

Appendix
References

---

## Unungak

An unungak was a type of harpoon used by Angulutiun and Iulutiun hunters.

Description
An unungak measured about  in length, featuring a sturdy wooden shaft and a razor-sharp bone tip. To enhance its strength, leather cords were tightly wound around the front of the shaft and securely fastened along its length. This meticulous binding added durability to the weapon, ensuring its reliability in demanding situations.

## Unnamed weretiger librarian

A scholarly weretiger, referred to as a librarian, that was active in the Sword Coast in the late 15 century DR.

Description
In his weretiger form the librarian wore a red tunic, a pair of spectacles, a belt around the waist that had books latched to it, footwraps, and a single leather bracer that had compartments for carrying three scrolls.

Personality
He considered his lycanthropy to be a blessing, rather than a curse, and would interject with this belief whenever anyone referred to it as being a curse.

History
Sometime around the,The character profile of Drizzt Do'Urden states his age to be 196 years old. Seeing as how he was born in 1297 DR (The Grand History of the Realms, p. 132), Idle Champions must be set in 1493 DR. That being said, the developers have stated they intentionally keep the game's timeline vague, to allow them to pick and choose the characters (or character versions) they want. this weretiger was living among a group of Beshaba worshiping lycanthropes that had taken over a ruined temple deep in the darkest reaches of the Neverwinter Woods, acting as the steward of the temple's library. The group went about afflicting the condition upon the locals, including Pavel Nonov.

One day a group of adventurers entered the Neverwinter Woods in search of a missing woman by the name Kara Bakovski. They encountered the hunter Randal Amberwood, who informed them that he had tracked beasts down to the abandoned temple. Once they made it to the temple the adventurers found Kara in the company of a werewolf and moved to attack it, but she yelled at them to stop and explained that the beast was her fiance. Introducing himself as Pavel Nonov, the werewolf expressed a desire to be cured of his lycanthropy. After expressing that they would need a scroll of remove curse, Pavel suggested the group might be able to find something in the temple's disorganized library. They rummaged through its shelves a bit, finding a copy of Volo's Guide to Lycanthropy, but once realizing that they weren't fellow lycanthropes the weretiger attacked them.

Having survived his encounter with the adventurers and with his group dismantled, the weretiger fled the Neverwinter Woods. He eventually made his way into the Arcane Chambers of Undermountain, seeking to research the lost lore held within books on that level. Unperturbed by the gibbering mouthers and nothics that inhabited the level, the weretiger continued his studies for well over a year.

Around the, a group of adventurers were traveling through the Arcane Chambers in search of Nihiloor and encountered several gibbering mouthers gathered around a bin of books. Their fighting garnered the attention of the weretiger librarian, irritated at the disturbance, and the two groups recognized each other from the temple back in Neverwinter Woods. The group pleaded that they had no quarrel with him, but having fallen under the sway of Undermountain's madness the weretiger lept into combat against the adventurers.

Unkra Forend was a half-orcish alchemist and herbalist for hire from the city of Neverwinter, active in the late 15 century DR.

History
Unkra was raised by an orcish clan somewhere outside of the city of Neverwinter. The clan taught her their relatively primitive herbalist skills and techniques. When Unkra was exposed to the modern advancements in herbalism and alchemy, she left her home and embarked on a quest to learn and master new techniques. The search led the half-orc to Neverwinter, where she settled and made a living by selling her alchemical services.

## Unfinished Business

Unfinished Business is a one round adventure module set in the Living City organized play program. It was published in May 1996 and was Part I of the Unfinished Business storyline. The module is intended for adventurers of any level.

Synopsis
As the High Murderer, once noble Anton D'Magus, now known as the Dark Lord, was brought to justice, a group of adventurers of the Living City failed to stop the enemy of Ravens Bluff. Soon after, the heroes are confronted by the mysterious chronomancer named Terra Moortain from the Dimension of Time. She showed the adventurers visions of the future that they unleashed – the Dark Lord Anton D'Magus would eventually come to claim Zhentil Keep as his own and lay waste to the Dalelands first, then to the Forest Kingdom of Cormyr, and eventually destroying the city of Ravens Bluff. To protect the Realms and fix the timeline, Terra sent the adventurers back in time to the very beginning of the adventure that led to Lord D'Magus's escape. The heroes retained no memory of the meeting with the chronomancer but would experience strong feelings of deja vu as they retreaded their steps. Unbeknownst to all, Anton D'Magus received a similar warning from another chronometer, and the future was already being changed. The adventurers of the Living City traveled to the Magus Keep to face the Dark Lord and bring him to justice. The confrontation went differently this way, and after a series of traps and an ambush, Anton was slain, the dark future of all Faerûn was undone.

## Unicorn language

The Unicorn language was the racial language of unicorns.

Speakers
Beyond unicorns themselves, Unicorn was a common secondary language among nymphs and many members of Faerûn's land-dwelling elven subraces, such as the lythari, moon elves, sun elves, and wood elves.

Unicorn was also fairly common as a secondary language among druids devoted to certain nature deities. These included the druids of Chauntea, druids of Eldath, druids and druid/rangers of Mielikki, the druids of Sheela Peryroyl, the druids of Shiallia, and the druids of Silvanus. Other forms of specialty priests in the Realms for whom Unicorn was a commonly known secondary language included the Eldathyn peacemen and the Shiallian woodwives.

## Undead graft

Undead grafts were body parts, organs, or other appendages formed of undead flesh that were grafted onto a living creature.

Description
Grafting the flesh of undead creatures onto oneself could confer tremendous powers depending on the piece and the creature from which it had been taken. Although animated by magic, such grafts did not radiate magic. If removed from a creature, they could not be reused.

Undead grafts were generally sought only by those who were desperately power-hungry or who revered undead. No sane person desired one, and any unfortunate individual who had one forced upon them was advised to find a way to remove it.

Creation
Creating undead grafts required vile procedures and pieces of bodies in perfect condition, often harvested from unwilling undead creatures. Attaching such an appendage (either to another or to oneself) required familiarity with medicine, access to spells relevant to the specific graft, specialized knowledge of how to attach such flesh to a body, and involved a complex and vile ritual. The procedure generally was conducted in a comfortable and quiet setting, such as a laboratory or evil temple.

The graft flesh spell could also be used to attach the limbs or skin of a corpse to a living creature, however this effect was only temporary.

History
Undead grafts were originally devised in experiments by necromancers and priests of death who sought to remake their bodies in the form of undeath without actually dying first.

As of the late 15 century DR, undead grafts could be purchased at the Corpse Market in Evernight from merchants such as Sanjos Irridan.

Notable Undead Grafts
Arms
Blazing skeleton claw The hand of a blazing skeleton which granted the ability to strike with fire.
Enervating arm This withered and leathery arm granted an enervating touch.
Ghostly arm An incorporeal limb that could not interact with physical objects but which granted a ghost's corrupting touch.
Mummified hand A withered hand that conferred strength and the ability to impose mummy rot on a victim.
Paralyzing arm A strong limb that granted a ghoul's touch.
Skeleton hand A skeletal claw that could be used to slash enemies.
Weakening arm A skeletal forelimb that conferred great strength.
Wight's claw A wight's hand granted the ability to drain the life from a victim.
Zombie arm Granted the uncanny strength of a zombie.

Head
Bodak's eye An empty white bodak eye that granted a once-daily gaze attack that could slay foes.
Eye of flame The red, eye-shaped gem from an eye of fear and flame enabled the recipient to cast fireball.
Lich's brain Granted the esoteric knowledge of a lich.
Mohrg's tongue The long, clawed tongue of a mohrg.
Mummy's eye Mummified eyeballs embedded into the eye socket could be used to instill fear or cast eyebite, but could not be used to look around.
Vampiric fangs Also known as the thirst of the vampire, some versions simply replaced the subject's teeth with those of a vampire while others also replaced parts of the digestive system to confer their blood-draining abilities.

Other
Bonemail Interlocking plates of bone grafted directly to a creature's body provided it with permanent armor.
Undead skin A protective, mottled gray hide that covered or replaced the original skin.

Notable Owners
 Bahldrak Slagspit and Bellegia Slagspit, dwarf assassin twins from Neverwinter who became living specimens for grafting experiments by the Red Wizards of Thay.

## Unseen spider servant

Unseen spider servant was a variation of the unseen servant conjuration spell that summoned an eight-legged arachnid to perform simple tasks at its user's orders.

Effects
Just like regular unseen servants, unseen spider servants were conjured creatures tasked with simple tasks. The creatures' adherence to the command and size could make them annoying as they tended to follow friendly guests, carrying dust cloths on each of their legs and cleaning guests' weapons and armor. Unseen spider servants were harmless but invulnerable to weapons and could only be dismissed through the dispel magic or similar spells.

History
The unseen spider servant spell was developed by Jalan Teng of the Green Robes, a wizard from the world of Oerth who ventured to Faerûn and, eventually, the Temple of Lolth near the trail that led to the town of Mossbridges in the Vast. By the, Jalan Teng was a restless spirit who made the abandoned temple of the Spider Queen his lair, laboratory, and place to keep his magical treasure hoard. Then Jalan's lair was disturbed by a band of adventurers of Ravens Bluff who investigated the lost ruin, where they encountered the restless spirit's unseen spider servant. It was tasked with cleaning the lair, which included the adventurers' armor. Jalan would get angry if one of his unseen spider servants were to be dispelled.

## Underwater boat

The underwater boat was an experimental non-magical self-propelled submarine developed by Nadul DaRoni.

Description
Nadul's device was a sealed one-person boat equipped with paddlewheels and a flipper and was slowly propelled by the pilot's hand-pedaling. It contained enough air for the pilot to breathe for at least ten or more minutes, depending on how well they controlled their breathing, and a set of releasable sandbags for ballast allowed the vehicle to surface from the depths (so long as it wasn't carrying more than  of passenger). Aside from the possibility of leaks, the vehicle was quite reliable and safe, especially compared to other inventions like the furnace wagon or heartland flier.

Availability
Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue offered the boat built by Nadul DaRoni.

## Undead slayer

Undead slayers were elves that devoted their lives to the cause of hunting down and combating undead.

Culture
Undead slayers came from all walks of life and all the elven subraces. The majority of undead slayers lived on mainland Faerûn, owing to the lack of undead on the elven island nation of Evermeet, though a few did live on the island in case an undead threat ever arose. They tended to be rather grim and serious-faced individuals, their chosen line of work having made them lose the lust for life that was typical of elves. They spent time studying the various patterns of undead existence and developed methods to combat them, but each went on to specialize in the knowledge of how to combat particular forms of undead.

Undead slayers were resolute in their mission. Chances to eliminate undead were never turned down or backed away from, regardless of what the personal cost might be. If sacrificing themselves would lead to the perishing of undead, slayers would not hesitate to take that cost. However, their feelings towards undead were not evenly spread. While mindless undead such as skeletons and zombies were no less worthy of being slain, undead slayers held particular animosity towards sentient undead, making no exceptions for those that had proven themselves to be friendly or harmless. For example, even if a lich that had proven itself to be a neutral good and kindly individual, their mere existence would infuriate undead slayers to no end and be cause enough for their destruction.Interestingly, the brief snippet on undead slayers in Elves of Evermeet brings up that baelnorns, an elven form of lichdom, are one of the few undead to exist on Evermeet and yet makes no mention of there being any hostility towards them from the few undead slayers that lived on the island.

Abilities
Undead slayers tended to be variously skilled in the fields of healing, herbalism, ancient and local history, ancient languages, and survivalism. Additionally, they learned how to hone the keen senses of the elven people to accomplish the unique feat of detecting the unnatural rhythms that most undead produced, though this ability couldn't detect the movements of vampires. Even without the use of this sense, undead slayers were knowledgeable enough of undead to notice signs of infestation and thereafter trace them back to their source.

Possessions
The commonly carried gear amongst undead slayers included two or more vials of holy water, a silver mirror, several sharpened stakes, multiple holy symbols, and tinder for torches. Amongst these items, holy water and a silver mirror were considered paramount starting points for any undead slayer's arsenal. In terms of weaponry, they typically carried a bludgeoning weapon, though sometimes complemented it with a piercing or slashing weapon. These weapons were always made of silver.

Notable Undead Slayers
 Argus Hawkmoon, a mage and cleric of Sehanine Moonbow.

## Undead servitor

Undead servitor was a type of arcane ritual available during the Spellplague era that allowed spellcasters to create undead.

Effects
When conducted, this ritual imbued the corpse of a humanoid that was native to the natural world, usually of demihuman or smaller stature, with life and an understanding of one language of the spellcaster's choice.The organized play module From One Dwarf to Another somewhat implies that this spell is not strictly limited to humanoid creatures, as in there a necromancer has created famine and rot hounds. However, the necromancer's activities are left ambiguous enough that it's possible he could have created these undead through separate means.

Generally, the undead created by ritual were rather basic, incapable of fighting, and only one of them could be active at a time. Any attempt to raise another undead through this ritual would simply fail whilst the first servitor was still active. However, some spellcasters found ways to circumvent these limitations.In the organized play module From One Dwarf to Another, the antagonist is stated to have been casting undead servitor on a daily basis and this implied to be the reason behind the varied types of undead servants that appear in the module and act as combatants against the player characters.

Components
The material components required to cast this ritual usually cost around 150 to 360 gold pieces.

History
Sometime prior to the Second Sundering, in the late 15 century DR, a male human necromancer from the Bleak Academy who was seeking to improve his necromantic craft began operating within an ancient Untheri burial site in Tymanther, near the frontier village of Ruinspoke. Deep within a mausoleum at the burial site, the necromancer discovered a vein of onyx. He began harvesting this vein, as well as excavating most of the tombs and burial sights in the area, for material components and body parts so he could cast undead servitor on a daily basis.It is not specified in the adventure whether the ruins being explored belonged to Unther or Tymanchebar. However, due to the adventure only making note of ruins of "the old Untheric Empire" and the presence of a Lathanderite sarcophagus in the mausoleum, it stands to reason the ruins were from Unther. The undead he created through this spell included two corpses of despair, three famine hounds, a zombie grave digger, a lasher zombie, and four rot hounds.

## Undril Silvertusk

Undril Silvertusk was a half-orc priestess of Torm and a representative of the Order of the Gauntlet who was active in  during the late 15 century DR.

Personality
Undril was a reasonable person. She was a strong proponent for those whom she respected, and was willing to stand up to her superiors in support of such people.

Possessions
She wore a chain shirt and was hesitant to part with this armor, even in conditions in which doing so was beneficial.

History
Around the time of the events of the death curse, Undril was tasked by her superiors in the Order of the Gauntlet with personally delivering a bundle of dispatches to Niles Breakbone, the leader of the Order's expedition to purge the undead from the jungles of Chult. Having been led to believe that she could simply acquire a horse in Port Nyanzaru and ride to his base at Camp Vengeance, she was caught off guard by the dense and dangerous jungle conditions upon her arrival in the Port. She thus sought to join any existing expedition that was already headed up the River Soshenstar, along which the camp was located.

## Uncle (wizard)

Uncle was what some called a particular reclusive wizard who owned a keep in the Kryptgarden Forest on the Sword Coast North. After his death, he left his keep to a relative who was either his niece or nephew.

Personality
The wizard known as Uncle was a recluse who did not maintain contact with people outside his keep, even family. Nevertheless, he was kind enough to treat his servants well, and made a point of celebrating their birthdays each year with parties. He was also conscious about security, as he filled his moat with invisible spears and had a secret escape route out of his keep.

Possessions
The wizard's most significant possession was Uncle's Keep, a modestly sized but well-decorated building in the southern end of the Kryptgarden Forest. In his keep, he kept a number of guards and servants. He also had a stable full of livestock, which included a pony.

He owned a magical mirror that could cause someone to be enthralled by their own reflection. He owned many other magic items, including enchanted weapons.

Activities
Before his death, the wizard lived a somewhat solitary life in his keep although he made regular visits to Red Larch.

Relationships
The wizard had few relatives. He was married to a woman from Red Larch, and had a friend who was a wandering druid.

He occasionally thought about his distant niece or nephew, to whom he was considered 'long-lost,' and who inherited his estate after his death.
His keep made him a target of the Lostafinga tribe of hobgoblins.

History
About five years before his death, the wizard saved the life of a lord of Waterdeep, who repaid him by granting him a tract of land in the south end of the Kryptgarden Forest, as well as a keep there and money.

Some time after, the wizard became married to a woman from Red Larch. His wife and their unborn child both died during childbirth.

Two years after the death of his wife, the wizard's keep was raided by a patrol of the Lostafinga tribe of hobgoblins. They killed his hirelings, and the wizard as well. As he bled out from a fatal arrow wound, his last act was to write a will in ink and his own blood, leaving the keep to a particular niece or nephew of his.

## Undraeth

Undraeth was a drow city in the Upper Darklands, beneath the Aphrunn Mountains  of southern Turmish. What little those on the surface knew about the city stemmed from two wars they had waged and lost with surface dwellers.

Geography
Tunnels connected the city westward to Oryndoll and northwest to Sshamath.

Government
Undraeth was ruled for several centuries by Queen Nathglaryst, a mage and Lolthite priestess. The demented ruler was worshiped by her subjects as an avatar of the Spider Queen herself.

Society
The Church of Lolth in Undraeth was populated by priestesses of only middle and low rankings, most of whom were direct descendants of Queen Nathglaryst. This spread of rankings were due to the Queen having no tolerance for perceived rivals to the absolute power she commanded. Consequently, few subjects dared to ascend in power, as it was said that any who did would inevitably find themselves as a sacrifice upon the altar of Lolth.

Relations
Undraeth had relatively good relations with the drow city of Guallidurth. Conversely, Undraeth and its army was in conflict with the duergar city of Underspires for several centuries.

History
In -1850 DR, the Underspires launched a series of attacks against various Underdark races, including the cities of Undraeth and Oryndoll.

In the, the city-state of Hlondeth was overran by a kobold army called the Tattered Cloth Legion. In the aftermath of the attack by the kobold army, it was discovered that the invasion was instigated by drow, dispatched by Queen Nathglaryst of Undraeth and led by Shilaris Shemwith, in an attempt to conquer it. Though the drow were unsuccessful in their attempt at conquest, Shilaris managed to abscond with the Hlondethan treasury, possibly hiding a portion of it in the sewers below the city and retreating to the Underdark with the remainder. From that day onward, Hlondeth prepared itself for an invasion from Undraeth that may never come.

Around the early 2 century DR, Undraeth in a profitable slaves trade with agents of the Red Wizards of Thay. Using pirates from the Sea of Fallen Stars, the Red Wizards had slave-caravans sent tiny anchorages along the coast of Turmish, thence they would be taken a short way to some mines that produced very little ore. The tunnels of those mines lead down to Undraeth in the Underdark. Eventually, some agents of the Harpers stumbled upon this operation while investigating unrelated Thayan slave-caravans and tried to put an end to the shipments. In retaliation, Queen Nathglaryst sent agents to the surface to slay the Harpers and hire folks that could. Though these hirelings were somewhat successful, they occasionally were captured or slain by members of the Magelords of Mintar, mistaking them for Harpers themselves.

Circa 1150 DR, the Harpers hired a mercenary army of their own to invade Undraeth. Anticipating that the Magelords of Mintar would use their conflict with Undraeth as an opportunity to strike at them, Khelben Arunsun and his compatriot Elminster made sure that all of the wizard members of the Harpers would be present for the invasion. On the day of attack, the assembled wizards used explosive spells to collapse Undraeth's mineshaft surface-link as the drow were scrambling up from the Underdark to confront them. With the loss of that surface-link and slave trade, Queen Nathglaryst's power base outside of Undraeth would crumble and her realm would further wither as it faced repeated skirmishes from Underspires.

In the, during the Time of Troubles, the avatar of the duergar god Deep Duerra appeared in Underspires in the form of the "Queen Mother", serving as regent until War King Olorn Ridaugaur came of age. Under her guidance the duergar assembled and trained an army of elite duergar warriors that were sent into forays against Ironfang Deep and Undraeth to shape them into the most battle-ready force that the Underspires had in centuries. Undraeth continued to be in conflict with them by 1370 DR and the Underspires had enslaved a large number of its drow citizens.

By 1371 DR, Queen Nathglaryst's focus was on reclaiming lost relics of Telantiwar, an ancient drow kingdom, convinced that doing so could help her to finally achieve her dream of a establishing an empire.

She dispatched agents throughout the Great Bhaerynden region of the Underdark, having them track down every lingering rumor of Telantiwar's relics and recover whatever still remained. The drow city of Llurth Dreier progressively noticed the Undraeth agents, whose presence posed an annoyance to its aboleth leaders, to the point that rumors arose that the Fanatics of the Overflowing Pit might be spurred to march to war and snuff out the self-styled City of the Spider Queen.

## Undevver Thort

Undevver Thort was a wizened retired adventurer turned shopkeeper, living in the city of Waterdeep in the late 15 century DR.

Personality
Undevver was known for having an odd sense of humor and a keen interest in new or strange artifacts. He especially took interest in any that had some sort of story attached to them.

Possessions
He was regularly seen walking around with and leaning upon a cane, which some local rumors suggested was "more than just a cane".

Activities
Beginning sometime after the rampage of the Walking Statues of Waterdeep in 1385 DR, Undevver lived in and ran the curios shop Thort's Findings out of the head of what once was the Walking Statue known as the Swordmaiden. He displayed the items in his shop with little placards written in his beautiful, flowing handwriting.

Relationships
Undevver was an ally of the Harpers organization. To aid their cause, he decked his shop out with hidden rooms to serve as safe-houses for Harper agents or Harper contacts.

## Unnamed pirate galley

The pirate galley, also known as the prison galley, was an unnamed typical Korinn Archipelago galley used by pirates as a slaveship. Its final destination was to be the pirate stronghold Westhaven, but it crashed on Viledel's Isle.

Description
It was about  long, with ten two-man oar benches down each side. As with any galley, it could carry a forty-man compliment of rowers, plus a captain and a steerman. It had a single mast and no forecastle or sterncastle. Its two hatches opened into the holds; the forward hatch opened into the forward hold and the aft hatch opened into a small aft hold. Before the crash, the aft hold held four barrels of wine and ale; dozens of sacks of grain; fifty quarrels for Hafkris' heavy crossbow, which was also stored there; a chest holding the Captain's Log (written in thieves' cant), navigational charts, and a spellbook containing two spells: magic missile and sleep.

The slave hold ran about two-thirds of the length of the galley, from the bow to about one-third of the way to the stern. A single hatch led to the deck.

After the crash
The port side of the galley was laid open by a huge boulder that the vessel had ground against. The sail was furled when the squall hit, and the mast snapped to come crashing down on deck mere minutes before the ship ran aground, blocking the single hold hatch. The barrels broke, spilling wine and ale everywhere; the sacks of grain were rendered useless by sea water and the barrels' content. The chest and its contents remained undamaged, as well as the crossbow and quarrel. If any of the survivors decided to use the wreck as shelter for the day, they would not live to see a new dawn as they would slowly freeze to death.

## Unholy halberd of chaos

Unholy halberd of chaos was an enchanted halberd that craved elvish blood. The weapon was of unknown but likely fiendish origins, created sometime before the early 14 century DR.

Powers
This hefty weapon was created to be wielded exclusively by individuals whose hearts were blackened by evil and chaos. The weapons possessed a +2 enchantment that guided its blade towards the target and made its slashes more powerful than similarly made mundane halberds. In addition to that, the weapon was especially deadly to elves, when wielded against them, it effectively became a powerful and deadly +4 halberd. It also dealt additional bludgeoning damage to elves, creatures of good and those who venerated law. And lastly, the unholy halberd sapped the life force from its enemies and healed the wielder with it.

History
Unholy halberd of chaos's history was unknown but it was believed that it must've once belonged to a foul fiend or a powerful undead creature, a vampire or a death knight. The halberd's desire for elvish blood led some to speculate that the unholy halberd of chaos once belonged to a drow weapon master from the Underdark city of Menzoberranzan.

In the, the halberd belonged to Agaza, the night hag who cursed the inhabitants of Lonelywood in Icewind Dale with sleepless nights and nightmares.

Appearances

## Undead lion

An undead lion was a fearsome undead lion brought to unlife by the mages of Thay.

Description
A Thayan undead lion was a skeletal creature with its bones being of glorious golden hue. It retained its mane and had long and deadly fangs protruding from the bony maw. Its eye sockets held two orbs with a sinister red glow. Undead lions were adorned with battle armor and a saddle painted with the mark of the Red Wizards of Thay.

Abilities
These creatures were built to nominate battlefields via their necrotic roar, which instilled fear into enemies' hearts and empowered their riders. When an undead lion unleashed its necrotic roar, it dealt psychic damage to the creatures that heard it, reduced the enemies' accuracy, made them vulnerable to all types of damage, and could leave them stunned and vulnerable.

History
By the late 15 century DR, Thayan undead lions were known dangers, and individuals with enough coin could purchase such a deadly creature to use as a mount and ride into battle atop it.

## Unfuwa

Unfuwa was a gnome sea mage who sailed upon the Ebony Queen in 1367 DR.

Activities
She was in the employ of Captain Soot.

Abilties
Spells known by Unfuwa included charm person, sea sight, wall of fog, ESP, true bearing, and stone hull. She was also knowledgeable about genie culture.

## Unilda Tiro

Unilda Tiro was a Black Gauntlet of Bane, who posed as a guard working for Baldur's Mouth in the Lower City of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

Possessions
Unilda was equipped with a chainmail uniform of Baldur's Mouth's guards and armed with a simple mace and wooden shield.

Abilities
As a cleric, Black Gauntlet Unilda could cast such spells as blight, dominate person, inflict wounds, phantasmal force, and summon a spirit guardians imbued with necrotic magics.

History
In the, Unilda Tiro was tasked with guarding the offices of Baldur's Mouth and its editor-in-chief, Ettvard Needle. She was tasked with guarding the elevator that descended into Baldur's Mouth's off-limits basement. None were allowed to pass without Ettvard's permission.
