## Unnamed Sarbreen cemetery

This small graveyard was an ancient burial site in Raven's Bluff, dating back long before it had been founded and incorporated into one city, back when the land was part of Sarbreen. By the 14 century DR, the graveyard had fallen into a state of disuse and neglect.

Location
This graveyard was located somewhere in the Crow's End district, near the south bank of the Fire River, close to some caves. It was boxed in on all sides by buildings, ones that were considered "old" by 1355 DR. Close by the graveyard was a sewer grate framed by granite stones.

Description
This graveyard covered an area of  x, with a perimeter surrounded by a  tall, iron fence. Set into the west side of this rusty fence was a small gate.

It was dotted with tombstones and statuary made of slate, as well as an abundance of weeds. Much of this statuary was in a decaying state by 1355 DR. Most of the tombstones had dates preceding the foundation of Ravens Bluff, save for two that were carved when the city was still relatively new.

One notable piece of statuary was a marble pedestal, topped with a marble carving of a nest, a few chicks, and a raven. It also bore a unique Dethek rune, the family sign of the Stoneshapers, an ancient dwarven clan. The statuary was a depiction of The Menagerie, an ancient relic which the Stoneshapers and other dwarven clans of the Vast had banded together to create. All together the piece weighed .

History
By the, some of the graveyard's coffins and their interred dead had fallen down into a forgotten dwarven building that had been bricked up when Ravens Bluff's sewer system was constructed. It was steeped in a  of brackish water, with coffins or bits of coffins floating in it, and the bottoms of coffins visible in the ceiling.

Sometime around 1355 DR, a group of adventurers were potentially jumped by a Zhentarim mercenary force under the employ of Erin Fleetwing and dragged into this cemetery. There they were threatened, interrogated about their relationship to Vernon Condor, and asked as to the exact nature of the mission they were undertaking for him. Once satisfied, the Zhents left with the valuables of the restrained adventurers, saying that some sort of spectre would feast upon them. When the adventurers eventually freed themselves and made it into the underground chamber next to the sewers, they observed that the bottoms of some coffins above them had been gnawed open and that at least one skeleton was pinned to the west wall. A set of granite stairs along the west wall lead up to an open archway, leading into an ancient  wide tunnel that had become infested with rats and small spiders.

Appendix
See Also
 Cemetery of Peace
 City Cemetery, the foremost graveyard of Ravens Bluff.

## Unicorn's Horn (Athalantar)

The Unicorn's Horn was a tavern near Athalantar in the early-to-mid 3 century DR.

Location
The Unicorn's Horn was a roadside inn in the middle of some sheep farms, about a day's walk from the mouth of the Delimbiyr River, due west of Athalantar.

Interior
They had flagstone floors, dim lighting in the taproom, and many quiet, private, dark corners. The tables had cup-holders.

Services
They were known to serve stew, wine, and roast.

History
In or before Flamerule of the, the Mystran priestess Elmara (secretly prince Elminster Aumar of Athalantar) went to the Unicorn's Horn after delivering Ondil's Book of Spells to Mystra's Dance at the mouth of the Delimbiyr. It took her a whole day's walk to get there. Once she arrived, she crossed paths with seven apprentice Magelords of Athalantar, who attacked a minstrel for singing the Ballad of the Stag, a song about Uthgrael Aumar. She stopped their spell, and then cast a spell that cut off all their magic. Rather than fighting them with spells, she asked all present if they'd like to brawl instead. In doing so, one of the magelords died, while the other six were knocked out. Elmara cast a spell that turned all of them to stone and then transported them to the waters near Mystra's Dance. According to her, once the spell wore off, they'd better swim fast.

Prince Gartos was later sent to capture her for these transgressions.

## Undarl's tower

Undarl's tower was a tower belonging to the mage Undarl in northwest Faerûn during the early-to-mid 3 century DR.

Location
The tower lay along the Delimbiyr River, just a few miles west of Secomber, or Hastarl in ancient times. It was built on top of a rocky outcropping.

Structure
The keep had open balconies both facing outside and inside, over the courtyard, with many major towers having flat roofs and being half-round, with the round side facing outwards. The base of the walls had plinths. The structure had six interior floors and three underground levels. The bottom-most level was too damp to use. All levels but the ground level, broken by the courtyard gate, made complete circuits around the structure.

However, most of the aboveground structure had been reduced to a jutting stone outcropping by the late 14 century DR, with only a basement laboratory preserved properly, with strong magic.

Inhabitants
Shadow creatures inhabited the surrounding area, due to a leaky portal.

History
The tower belonged to Undarl in its day, but had since become a ruined keep.

Appendix
See Also
 Hornkeep

Background
Ed Greenwood has likened the tower to the real castle of Pierrefonds.

## Unmar Halberg

Unmar Halberg was a dwarf inhabitant of Eveningstar and the town stonemason during the late 15 century DR.

Description
Unmar had light skin and long bright orange-red hair, his beard braided in a fork.

History

During the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar, Unmar was distressed to find that the Market Square well was poisoned. While the Purple Dragons and War Wizards were busy in battle, he sent brave adventurers down into the underground caverns that fed the town's water supply. Down there they found yuan-ti agents hired by the drow to poison the town, overcoming maddened dryads, the yuan-ti leaders Saz-sass, Gresl-glis, and Fless-onas, and even their allied green dragon Tharmalos.

## Underdark Arena

The Underdark Arena was a gladiatorial arena in the Underdark of Cormyr during the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar in the late 15 century DR.

Deescription

The arena was a large square chamber with gates on each side, nooks in each corner, and a slightly elevated stage in the center. The audience and the arena master viewed the fights from their seats above the arena.

History

When the drow attacked the King's Forest and Eveningstar, House Cay'Vorym aimed to profit by using captured surfacers in their arena fights. Overseen by Azena'ar the drider, they ran fights to the death with willing drow and yuan-ti contestants, and large numbers of enslaved human villagers, Purple Dragons, and War Wizards controlled with drow slave collars. With the duergar Djir Garpeth advertising the events in the Eveningstar Cavern, they hoped to draw in opportunistic willing fighters from the surface as well. Azena'ar's champions included Oolgnim the oneirophage, Hessperina the medusa, and several powerful drow warriors, mages, and priestesses. There was also a mysterious wizard who donated treasure chests to the arena—though each one was accompanied by three mimics. If all the champions were defeated, Azena'ar may have been incensed enough to jump into the arena himself and fight to the death

Some time after this, the red dragon Aptoryx took over the arena, filling it with molten magma and replacing most of the gladiators with summoned planar beings that could survive the heat. His stable of champions included Scintilla the Fire Witch, the hulking and upset Brimstone Archon, and the salamander Finnfaran. The unseen wizard also returned to provide a mix of chests and mimics. If all his fighters were destroyed, Aptoryx would be intrigued enough to risk his life fighting in the arena as well.

## Underwater Realm of the Wizard

The Underwater Realm of the Wizard was a large sunken peninsula on the island of Flamsterd within the Moonshae Isles.

Geography
Once a promontory that comprised nearly half of the southern side of Flamsterd, the peninsula itself had been rendered into a wasteland by magic before exploding and sinking beneath the surface of the Trackless Sea.

History
The Underwater Realm was created in the, when the wizard Flamsterd moved his tower beneath the waves so that he could pursue his magical studies in solitude. He sent away his warring apprentices and enlisted the aid of druids to help him with the preparations, but the result was the apparent explosion of his tower and of the entire peninsula. The southern half of his island was flooded as the sea rushed in, becoming the Underwater Realm, while the northern half was left bleak and barren. Flamsterd survived as planned inside his tower, which was encased in a massive bubble of air at the bottom of the sea. The submerged realm came to be seen as a place of intrigue for daring adventurers.

Flamsterd was thought dead for many decades before once again inviting apprentices to serve him in the mid–14 century DR. While the name "Underwater Realm of the Wizard" had originally referred only to the area of Flamsterd's tower, it had come to refer to the whole underwater region by the late 15 century DR.

Notable Locations
 Flamsterd's Sunken Tower

## Unicorn rider

Unicorn riders were an elite cavalry of Queen Amlaruil Moonflower and the defenders of Evermeet, notable for riding unicorns.

Activities
Unicorn riders served Queen Amlaruil as warriors in her army on Evermeent, like the elven knights, but some could also be found in Faerûn, where they operated as agents of Evermeet or simply as adventurers and mercenaries. Those on Evermeet could be mobilized for defense at a moment's notice.

Culture
For reasons unknown, only elven women could become unicorn riders. Each spring, elven shield-maidens ventured into the forests in hopes of attracting a unicorn, which might agree to serve as their mount for a year. On average, a hundred to several hundred unicorn riders resided on Evermeet at any given time.

An elf chosen by a unicorn was regarded with near-religious respect and awe by the other elves of Evermeet, and many riders went on to become priestesses once their service ended. When unicorn riders visited their home cities or villages, they were met with admiration and celebration, as everyone wished to touch and interact with the unicorn. Defending Evermeet was regarded as the sacred duty of these riders. These unicorns initially agreed to serve their riders for a year. However, some chose to remain with their riders indefinitely if they were treated with respect.

The unicorns of Evermeet were regarded as especially unique, for the elves believed them to be the direct descendants of Eachthighern, the god of unicorns and pegasi, and that he would return to defend them should they be endangered. Unicorns were common in the forests of Evermeet, and the elves held them to be sacred. To kill a unicorn was an unthinkable act the elves, and any visitor who harmed or slew one would hunted down and killed without mercy.

Abilities
Unicorn riders had at least above-average physical and mental traits.

They were adept at grooming horses and were proficient in using lances and all types of bows. They could also become proficient in any type of mounted weapon. They were proficient in training animals and riding land-based creatures, including unicorns. It was also good for unicorn riders to possess animal handling, orienteering, hunting, and survival skills. After becoming a unicorn rider, an individual had an easier time communicating with unicorns in general. Unicorn riders could also freely communicate with their unicorn.

History
A cavalry of 50–100 unicorn riders existed in ancient Cormanthyr as part of the Wing and Horn cavalry of the Akh'Velahr.

The traditions and practices of Evermeet unicorn riders were well established by the Year of the Shield, 1367 DR.

Notable Unicorn Riders
 Ialantha Rumithamal, a cousin of Queen Amlaruil and formerly a unicorn rider.
 Sister Iahalae, an elf woman from Aerilaya's tribe on Evermeet who became a unicorn rider.

Appendix
See Also
 Knights of the Unicorn, an adventuring party, and later a mercenary company that derived their name from their service to Lurue, the Unicorn Queen.

References

## Undead hunter

Undead hunters were paladins who trained to be especially effective against undead opponents, albeit at the cost of neglecting their healing capabilities.

Abilities
These warriors learned to more effectively target, damage, and defend against undead creatures. They were immune to hold person-like effects and their experience in battle could not be affected by the powers of other creatures.

Because of their training regiment, they could not lay on healing hands.

They could turn undead like clerics, but with less power. With training, they could cast protection from evil and detect evil as well as other divine spells.

The most powerful undead hunters could use a myriad of martial techniques, along with war cries and could even summon a deva for aid in battle.

Appendix
See Also
Deathslayers, human mages that similarly devoted themselves to wiping out undead.
Necrobanes, Kelemvorite specialty priests similarly devoted to wiping out undead.
Undead slayers, elves that devoted their lives to the cause of hunting down and combating undead.
